    A more vexed question is that of spellings. The Persian documents tend
to follow certain Indo-Persian conventions, such as writing zunnardar for
Brahman, or baqqdl for Banya, or La‘l (with the Arabic ‘ain) for Lal. These
should perhaps cause little trouble. Further, the Persian documents follow
the local pronunciation. Thus, they consistently spell ‘Bindraban’, which
used to be the popular local form of the name (besides the fully Prakrit
‘Bindaban’), as is shown by the Braj/ Rajasthani texts as well. As the Braj
documents often show, the local pronunciation of Indic words sometimes
diverged even further from the Sanskrit forms than the spelling used in
Persian documents. Thus, we have Braj ‘Se Ram’ and Pers. ‘Seo Ram’, for
the Sanskrit ‘Shiva Rama’. The short vowel ‘a’ usually placed at the end of
a Sanskrit word is, of course, dropped, as in Hindustani; and compound
consonants are avoided or distorted: thus we have Kisan or Kishan for the
 Sanskrit Krishna. The consonants ‘j’ and ‘y’ are always interchangeable;
and so are ‘s’ and ‘sh’, thus ‘Acharaj’ for ‘Acharya’, The Sanskrit consonant
‘v’ tends to change into ‘b’ so much that the Nagari letter for ‘v’ has a
dot placed under it in Braj /Rajasthani documents if that consonant is
 intended, and not ‘b’. To restore the Sanskrit forms of personal names
would seem both pedantic and misleading; and I have simply tried to
 follow the spellings most used in the documents. Thus, for example, I have
 mostly written Gosa’in or Gusa in (the usual spellings in our documents,
 besides Gosa’i, or even Gusa’i), not Gosvamin or Goswami.
    The arrangement by names below follows the spellings of personal
 names as given in our documents and adopted by us. Where the name
 occurs in any Braj document, it is normally assumed to be the form that
 was actually in use. Names occurring in Persian documents alone, whose
 Braj forms are not available, are adopted as they are spelt. However,
 the short vowels, omitted in Persian writing, are restored according to
 presumed Braj forms: thus Gusain’, not ‘Gasa’in. No attempt is made to
 resort to the Sanskrit forms of names, unless they actually occur in our
 documents. The reader who wishes to trace a particular name in this list
 is requested to bear these complexities in mind, and to look always for
 alternative possibilities, for the documents are by no means consistent
 in their spellings of personal names: for example, we have the spellings
 Kishan and Krishan side by side in the same document, while we have
  Shiv Ram in one and Seo Ram in another.
 194                   Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

   The only departures from the practice of following spellings as found
in our records have been the frequent use of the spelling ‘Brindaban’ in
place of‘Bindraban’ of our records, and the substitution of the older and
established names of Mathura and Vrindavan for the names respectively of
Islamabad and Mominabad in documents after 1670.
   A name given in capital letters within the text of an article indicates
that the reader is advised to consult the entry under that name for further
or supplementary information; the same is also indicated by putting the
abbreviation ‘q.v.’ after the name of a person to whom a separate entry is
assigned.

BRAJANAND, Swami, s/o Gobind s/o Shiv Ram (Seo Ram) (q.v.);
also adopted s/o Gobind Charan (q.v.) s/o Raghunath.
1704 Parwdna of Mukhtar Khan, Nazim (Governor) of suba Agra, 1
Shawwal 48 Aur. (7 February 1704): Swami Brajanands chain of preceptors
(piran ptr) goes back to Rup (q.v.) & Haridas (q.v.), who established the
rules of the mendicants of their sect. They are recluses, who have no
(worldly) desires, and who engage in the worship of Divinity, undertake
pilgrimages within Braj Bhum; they do not pursue mendicancy (gadai)
residence outside Braj Bhum; & they entertain all mendicants, Hindu &
Muslim, who arrive hungry. Conformable to previous practice, & with
agreement of the zamindars of all the parganas of Braj Bhum, a rupee
from each village is to be paid in the year, half of it at each harvest, to
Brajanand, representing ‘the heirs of Rup & Haridas’, without loss of
State revenue; this to be done regularly. The 18 parganas from which
these levies are to be paid are then listed: (a) Mathura & other pargs. on
‘this* (right) side of Jamna R: Mathura, Sahar, Mangotla, 01, Au, Paharl,
Kaman, Khoh, Noh, Khohri, Hodal, Palwal, & Faridabad. (b) ‘That’ (left)
side of Jamna: Mahaban (including the tappa of Mat), Sa'dabad, Jalesar,
Koi, & Anupnagar.

BRAJ JANANAND / BRAJ-jANAND, s/o Nitanand s/o Radhanand.
Fifth in the line of Samanand (SYAMANAND); also his grandson or

    1703 Came from Orissa in 47 Aur./vs 1759 (=ad 1703) to Brindaban
bringing for Gobind Charan (q.v.) offerings (nazr o niydz) of cash & kind,
as befitted a disciple. Represented to Gobind Charan, who had custody
of the [Amber] Raja’s lands, that Syamanand’s small garden (baghicha)
was not sufficient for accommodating the Bairagis; & so Gobind Charan
allotted him 12 biswas of land ‘on lease’ (‘ariyatan) out of the Garden
(Bagh) of Chirghat, adjacent to Syamanand’s garden, from the Raja’s amldk
(grant-land), for the accommodation of the visiting Gaudiya mendicants,
according to established custom.
   Thereafter Braj Jananand started for home & after some years died
there. He left behind his servant & agent, Nand Kishor (q.v.), who, after
his death, in the reign of the martyred emperor (Farrukhsiyar), during
the regime of the traitorous Saiyid Brothers (namak-hardmdn), usurped
the land (Statement after qdzis judgement, c. 1723: GD). Braj Jananand
therefore must have died well before 1719. Also see an earlier statement
on behalf of Jagannath (CU, of 1723). A document of gift (hibanama)
produced before the qdzi showed that Braj Jananand had received from
Gobind Charan 12 biswas of land as gift (hiba), and not on lease; but this
was (in 1723) held to be illegal since Gobind Charan, not being the owner,
could not gift away the land owned by the Amber ruler (Qazi’s judgement
of 5 Muh. 1136: GD, copy in Jai).

(Wright 27, 17 June 1736). A sign of another phase of reconciliation

BRINDAVAN DAS, adhikari, s/o Narsinghdas adhikari.
1734 'Belonging to the line (pariwar) of Kisandas Kabiraj Gusai’ (q.v.
Kabiraj), buys land in Vrindavan, with Vrajanand Rai or Brajanand (q.v.)
being among those whose kunj and land bordered the plot purchased (vs
1791: Wright 26).

CHAITANYA
Referred to as ‘our Gur(u) Kishan Chaitan’ by Gauranga Bangali (q.v.), in
connexion with a dispute with one Damodardas Radhavallabhi (statement
before the Qazi of Mathura, ah 1064/ad 1654).
   It is strange that this is the sole reference to Shri Chaitanya in the
Vrindavan documents examined by us. He is nowhere credited with either
discovering Vrindavan or initiating Krishna worship there, the latter
achievement being attributed to RUP and SANATAN.

CHANDAR SEN s/o Radha Ballabh.
1718 Continues to hold imperial grant of 30 bighas in in dm in vill. Hasang,
Parg. Mahaban, originally conferred by Akbar in 1576 upon Bhagwan Das
(q.v.), and continuing with his heirs (Hasbul Hukm, Far. 7: IVS 157, zimn
only).
   1724 As wakil of Gopal (apparently GOPAL DAS s/o Anand Das,
priest of Gopinath temple), Chandar Sen petitioned the Diwan of Suba
Agra that, by Akbar’s farman, his client held 30 bighas of land as in‘am
in vill. Daharwa, parg. Mahaban, but the farman Sc former sanads were
lost in ‘the Jat turbulence’ (fasad-i Jat), a public endorsement (mahzar) to
this effect being in his posssession. Officials were directed not to disturb
Gopal’s grant, while all income from it, harvest after harvest, should be
                  A Documentary History of the Gosains                 203

received by Chandar Sen (Qazi-certified copy of‘All Naqi Khan s parwdna,
7 Muh: IVS 181).
   1731 As agent (karindd) of Ram Jiwan Gusain, q.v., appeared before
the qazl of Mathura to contest occupation of a plot of land belonging to
Gopinath temple at Vrindavan, by one Devaki Nandan, ‘Sarsat’ Brahman
(IVS 158)
   Apparently, Chandar Sen was connected with the establishment of the
Gopinath temple, Vrindavan.

CHAND KUNWAR, CHAND KUMAR.
Fourth in line of Jiv Gosain (q.v.), successor of Krishan Das (q.v.)
(statement on behalf of Jagannath, c.1723: CU).
   Since the last available date for his predecessor, Krishan Das is 1659,
and the earliest reference to Braj Kunwar (q.v.), though not necessarily as
Chand Kunwar’s immediate successor, is 1669, Chand Kunwar probably
held his office during the 1660s.

Khans parwdna, NAI-2671/9).

1608 Writes, & signs as first witness, the codicil (of vs 1665) to the will

Diwan, the agents of the faujdar of Kaman, & Hasan Beg, the Kotwal
(of Mathura), & the agent of the Waqd’i‘ Nigdr (of Kaman?). Copy of
decision, NAT2671/18.

GOPINATH

 Sisters (female cousin’s?) son of Gopi Raman (q.v.) & daughter’s son of
 Braj Kunwar (q.v.) (Ghairat Khans dastak of 5 Bah., CU, & Statement of
 c.1718 RD),
    1706. Gopinath received a small part of properties & rights when
 Kishan Priya (q.v.) gave away the main rights and major part of properties
 to Gopi Raman, q.v. (NAI 2671/19 of vs 1763). For details see KISHAN
 PRIYA.
    It was alleged later that Gopinath was brought from Bengal by Kunj
 Das (q.v.), who pretended that he was the true successor of Braj Kunwar,
 q.v., although in the Gaudiya sect a daughters son could never succeed to
 a spiritual seat (bil. statement of c.1718: RD).
    1710 Along with Gopinath, Kunj Das appeared before the qazi of
Mathura, claiming to represent Kishan Priya & stating that she had made
a gift (hibd) of 12 haveIts, gardens, etc., in Brindaban, Kaman, Arith,
Nandesar, & Govardhan, to Gopinath, her daughters son. In the personal
description, Gopinath’s age is not recorded, but he is said to have reached
full height (tamdm qad).
    1711 He & Kunj Das were trying to seize haveli         land of the Raja
of Amber, whose custody had been entrusted by Raja Jai Singh to Gopi
Raman (q.v.) & Brajanand (q.v.) (Ghairat Khan’s dastak of 5 Bah.: CU),
& an earlier parwdna of Mu‘tamad Khan to [Imtiyaz Mahal B egam’s
amln,] Shaikh Abu Said, 5 Bah.: CU.
    1712 By now dead, after his supporter Kunj Das had secured by
misrepresentation (khilafnumdi) a sanad (from the Imperial Court) in his
favour as custodian of the property of the Amber ruler (Raja Jai Singhs
parwdna, ah 1124: CU). A statement, c.1718, tells us that Gopinath had
died from an attack of smallpox (RD, undated). See also KUNJ DAS.
    1713 Accused, with Kunj Das, of claiming on basis of forged sanads that
he had the custody of the imperial grant-land of 135 bighas, and havells &
gardens on lands of Raja Jai Singh, obtained by purchase in Brindaban,
the custody of which the Raja himself had entrusted to Brajanand &

GOPINATH JI
 1608 Witnessed codicil to Skt. will of Jiv Gosa in (q.v.) (BSOAS, XLII

GOPI RAMAN / RAWAN Gosain, s/o Damodar Raman [s/o Nand
Kunwar].
Sixth in Jiv Gosa’ins line, brother’s son & successor of Braj Kunwar (d.
1698-1700) (q.v.). (Statement, c.1725: CU; & statement, c. 1718: RD).
   1706 Having come from Bengal in response to letter from Braj Kunwar’s
widow, Kishan Priya (q.v.), was installed by her, according to ‘Hindui’
custom, as possessor of his adhikdr 8c seats (ja-ha), through gift (hiba) by
her, at the end of Sha‘ban 50 Aur./16 Dec. (Statement, c.1718: RD). The
original Braj text of Razindma (deed of agreement) is extant by which
Kishan Priya assigned the major part of properties & rights possessed by
her husband Braj Kunwar to his brother’s son (bhatija), Gopi Rawan (so
spelt), while assigning a minor share of properties & rights to Gopinath
(q.v.) (for details see KISHAN PRIYA) (Nagari text of rdjinama, NAI-
2671/19, of vs 1763).
   c.1710 Some years later when Gopi Raman asked for accounts from
Kunj Das (q.v.), who had been managing the establishment (kdrkhana)
of his predecessor and had possessed himself of all money & goods, Kunj
Das summoned Gopinath (q.v.) from Bengal, & sought to supplant Gopi
Raman by Gopinath through obtaining sanads under false pretences,
although ‘from the time we have settled in this village after cutting
down the jungle, there has been no case of a daughters son getting the
inheritance’ (public statement, c. 1718: RD).
   1710 (1) Gopi Raman’s cause was abandoned by Kishan Priya as
well, because on 7 August Kunj Das appeared before Qazi Qutbuddin
on her behalf to record her gift (hiba) of all her properties, in havelis,
lands, & gardens at Brindaban, Kaman, Arith, Nandesar, & Govardhan
to Gopinath, her daughters son (Statement before Qazi, ah 1122: RD).
This gift apparently included a haveli 8c some land in Vrindavan which
belonged to Raja Jai Singh, 8c it was around this that the dispute raged
subsequently, since Gopi Raman claimed to be the custodian [of this part]
of the Rajas possessions at Vrindavan.
   (2) Vrijanand (Brajanand, q.v.), representing Gusai Jagannath (q.v.),
petitioned Raja Jai Singh of Amber that Gopi Raman, adhikari of Radha
212                   Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

Damodar temple, desired that a parwdna be issued to let him stay in the
retreat (kunj) where Braj Kunwar (q.v.) used to stay, within the 135 bighas
of imperial grant-land held by the Ruler of Amber, at Vrindavan. This
was because he was the late Braj Kunwars brother’s son & successor. Plea
granted (Parwdna of Jai Singh: H, pp. 205-07, of vs 1767).
   1711 Gopi Raman & Brajanand, claiming to hold custody of all
land, havelis, gardens, etc., possessed by Raja Jai Singh in Vrindavan,
complained that although Gopi Raman was in occupation of part of the
land in the Rajas haveli, by his sanad [see sub-entry (2) under 1710] &
written agreement (razd-ndma) of Braj Kunwar’s widow, Kishan Priya,
yet Kunj Das Kayath, & Gopi Ramans sisters son, Gopinath, had taken
possession of all of Braj Kunwars goods and were trying to seize possession
of the haveli as well. The amin (revenue assessor) and two named chobdars
(mace-bearers) of the Nazirs court respectively were directed to restore
the property to rightful possessors (Mu'tamad Khan’s parwdna to amin of
Imtiyaz Mahal, CU; & Ghairat Khan’s dastak of 5 Bah: CU). Apparently,
Mathura was then in the jdgir of Princess Imtiyaz Mahal.
    1712 Gopi Raman’s claims to haveli & land within the Amber estate at
Vrindavan were now upheld by Raja Jai Singh as against the pretensions of
Kunj Das on behalf of Gopinath, now dead (vide his parwanas to Bichauli
Jagjivandas and two other officials, wih the same text and date. CU: ah
1124).
   1713 (1) Petitioned, along with Brajanand, against Gopinath’s
pretensions over custody of Raja Jai Singhs grant of 135 bighas and other
land, haveli, & gardens at Mominabad (Vrindavan) (Qutbu’l Mulk Saiyid
‘Abdullah Khan’s parwdna of 2 Far: G.D; & another of same minister,
same date (copy): CU).
   (2) Dastak very similar in contents to those of Ghairat Khan (of 1711),
issued again in favour of Gopi Raman, on petition of Brajanand, by
Munlm Khan (RD 118 of 2 Far.)
   Gopi Raman thus succeeded in obtaining orders in his own favour, &
getting Kunj Das & Gopinath evicted from the haveli on Raja Jai Singh’s
land at Brindaban. For this he had to pay much money, & had obtained
assistance of Shaikh Abu Said, amin of Princess Imtiyaz Begam. Gopinath.
then died of smallpox. Kunj Das, though a Kayasth [& not a Brahman],
thereupon displayed forged sanads and pretended to have succeeded to
the headship becoming vacant on Gopinath’s death and had now taken
possession of the disputed haveli (Statement, undated but prob. c. 1718 on
behalf of‘all Gosains, sants,   panch" of Vrindavan: RD).
   1719 Gopi Rawan Das Gusain complained that some people were
trying to usurp the lands that his ‘ancestor’ Jiv Gusa in had acquired by
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa'ins                 213

purchase in Brindaban. Local officials were directed to protect his rights
(‘Abdullah Khan Qutbu’l Mulk, 1 Muh: RD).
    1720 (1) Complained against Nathu Ram, a Brahman of Mathura, that
he was trying to seize & build a walled enclosure (ahdta) in the land
that Gopi Raman had inherited from Jiv Gosa’in at Arith/Radhakund
(Parwdna of X to Badan Singh (father of the famous Jat chief Suraj Mai),
2 Muh.: CU; two parwanas of Ghairat Khan, 2 Muh. RD & MM; copy
Qiparwdna of Qutbu’l Mulk, 2 Muh: RD 18; Chauramans parwdna, year

to Nathu Ram, designated ‘Brahmmurat Nirmal Gangajal, etc.’, asking
him to suspend the construction of his kunj at Arith (RD): also parwdna
of Thakur Badan Singh, 2 Muh., CU).
    (2) Parwdna of Sikandar Singh (apparently, jagirdar), granting 40 bighas

(1720-1): RD 31.
    1721 Complained against intruders who were cutting down trees in
land which he had inherited from his ‘ancestor’ Jiv Gusa’in, who had
purchased it & established tank & gardens therein, at Arith/Radhakund
(Copy: Burhanu’l Mulk Sa‘adat Khans parwdna, 3 Muh. MM).
    1722 (1) Gopi Rawan Das complained that Nand Kishor (q.v.), resident
of Brindaban, had seized the haveli of Kishan Priya (q.v.), wife of Braj
Kunwar, the complainant’s uncle. Kishan Priya had proved her rights to
it and recovered it; but now Nand Kishor & Gobind Charan (q.v.) have
taken possession of the haveli, with cash & goods within it, closed the old
entrance & opened an entrance towards their own lands. Inquiry & relief
ordered (Nizamu’l Mulk’s parwdna, 4 Muh.: orig. CU, copy RD).
   (2) Also complained that his ‘ancestor’ Jiv Gusa’in had purchased land
in Bindraban, on which Kishordas Rajavat, kotwal of Amber, had raised
some buildings that he gifted to the complainants uncle, Braj Kunwar, but
now the Syamanandis, Gobind Charan & Nand Kishor, misrepresenting
the facts, were trying to usurp the land. Inquiry & relief ordered (Nizamu’l
Mulk’s parwdna, 4 Muh.: RD).
   Note: It is curious that Gobind Charan should be mentioned in both
these documents as a current colluder with Nand Kishor, when he was
dead by 1713. The explanation seems to be that these complaints were
an echo of the complaint of Kishan Priya (q.v.) against Gobind Charan
(q.v.) and Nand Kishor (q.v.) in 1707 so that Gobind Charan was deemed
to have been a colluder. See especially entry for NAND KISHOR for this
dispute.

    (3) Obtained a parwana from a high official (seal illegible) requiring
officials of pargana Sahar to protect his property (a garden and haveli)
that he had inherited from Jiv Gosa’in (exact place not stated, presumably
Arith). (VRI, SI. No. 52, Acc. No. 5, of 4 Muh)
    1724 Complained of theft committed in his haveli of ornaments, gold,
silver, pearls, cloth, utensils, etc. Thieves had twice previously taken away
some utensils, cloth, one ox and cart (6 Muh. RD 80 = VRI SI. No. 62,

    1725 (1) Gopi Raman, describing himself as s/o Damodar Raman,
acknowledges his obligation to pay Rs. 30 to Qazi Nur Muhammad for
having procured sanad of imperial grant of 50 bighas from Imperial offices.
Promises to pay from the proceeds of the kharif season (Bond, 7 Muh.:
VRI, SI. No. 53/Acc. No. 9).
    (2) Jagannath complained against Gopi Raman & Kunj Das of using
three forged farmans in favour of their preceptors (who in their lifetimes
never showed awarenss of them & whose genuineness is not confirmed
from imperial office records) to evict him and other mendicants from their
seats at Brindaban. Officials ordered to give protection to the complainant
and to seize the forged farmans (order of the Vazir Qamaruddin Khan to
All Naqi Khan Rasikh, 7 Muh.: copy GD, Rasikh’s own parwana (GD)
thereon, also a hasbu’l hukm to Raja Jai Singh, to obtain a statement of
agreement (razindma) between the parties 7 Muh.: (GD).
    (3) Upon complaint that Nand Kishor, a murid of Syamanand, had
made use of his residence in a kunj, to defy the authority of Gopi Raman,
who had inherited the kunj from Jiv Gosa’in, Raja Sawai Jai Singh ordered
Nand Kishor to be evicted from the kunj, which was to be restored to Gopi
Raman (Jai Singhs parwana, 7 Muh: RD). This seems to have altered Jai
Singhs position in relation to the rightful claimant, since, until 1724, he
was requiring Nand Kishor to vacate the kunj and land he had occupied,
so that these could be handed over to Jagannath (see NAND KISHOR
SYAMANANDI).
    (4) At the directions of Raja Sawai Jai Singh, the ndlh faujdar, Kirpa
Ram, enquired into the rights to the kunj of Braj Kunwar, and, then,
expelling Nand Kishor, installed therein Gopi Raman Gosain, murid
(follower) ofJiv Gosa’in (Gopi Raman’s statement, for public endorsement
as mahzar, undated: CU, copy: RD).
    (5) A public statement by Gopi Raman, calling for witnesses {‘gosa’ins,
mahajans, chaudhuris, qdnungss, zamindars, and common inhabitants of
village Bindraban’), to the effect that Syamanandis, who had lived in
a kunj left behind by Jiv Gosa’in, in time became defiant, and so Braj
                   A Documentary History of the Gosains                    215

Kunwar (q.v.), of the line of Jiv Gosain’, wanted to evict Radha Krishan
Das, disciple of Syamanand, from the kunj, but relented at his abject
submission. But later, Nand Kishor in collusion with Brajanand (q.v.)
concocted false papers in order to take possession of the kunj in Mukhtar
Khan’s time (as Governor of Agra, last years of Aurangzeb). Kunj Das,
‘one of the followers of Jiv Gosa in’, at last succeeded in getting Nand
Kishor Syamanandi expelled, closing the gate of the kunj. But now Nand
Kishor has got the gate reopened and occupied the kunj despite it being
lawfully a part of the peroperty left behind by Jiv Gosa in (original with
witnesses’ endorsements, CU; copy, RD 27). Undated, but from contents,
prob. 1725.
   In an undated doc., extant only in copy, Gosain Braj Lal (q.v.)
describes himself as son of Gopi Rawan, ‘adhikdri of Jiv Gosa’in’, himself
a resident of Brindavan, and claimant to property and rights acquired by
Jiv Gosa in at vill, Arith (CU: copy). Braj Lal was, therefore, a direct heir
and successor of Gopi Raman.

HARDAT, Pujari of Radha Rawan temple, Vrindavan, s/o Bansidhar
Gosa’in.
1706 Complained to Prince Jahan & Jahdniyan (Mu‘azzam) through
his sevaks against Gobind Charan (q.v,), pujdra {pujari) of Gobind-Dev
temple, for having occupied his land {zamin-i milkiyat). The Prince
ordered the Nazim (Governor) of Ajmer to send a sazdwal (mace-bearer)
to examine the papers. Bijai Singh (of Amber) secured suspension of
this process by assurances of enquiry & justice to Hardat. He issued a
parwana, dated 1 Muharram 1118/15 April 1706 (GD), addressed to two
persons, apparently of some religious status, one being designated purohit,
to check the relevant papers and restore the land to Hardat, ‘Pujara of Sri
Radha Rawan Ji’, if he is the rightful claimant.
   Note: The parwana by Bijai Singh issued at this time, when he did
not yet calim to be the ruler of Amber, indicates that he was then posted
                  A Documentary History of the Gosdi’ins                217

in the retinue of Prince Mu‘azzam (Bahadur Shah) and so was able to
intervene in the matter.

With Rup, he settled at Brindaban, which was full ofjungle & uninhabited,

    1724 Maharaja Sawai Jai Singh asked two Gosa’ins, Syam Charan
(q.v.) (of Gopinath temple) and Subal (=Subal Das, q.v., of Madan
Mohan temple) to settle with Gosa in Jagannath the terms on which the
dispute with Nand Kishor could be resolved. Subal was away at Karauli,
so Syam Charan on his own had the entrance of the kunj (occupied by
Nand Kishor) closed. Jagannath did not agree with this (as sufficient) and
demanded that either (a) Nand Kishor should keep his land, but surrender
the land of the sarkar (of the Amber ruler), while either accepting
monetary compensation for the material of structures (^amla) he had put
up on that land or carrying it away; or (b) he may continue to stay in the
kunj at the pleasure of the sarkar, but undertake to leave the kunj on the
above terms the moment Jagannath so desired. Further, letters exchanged
on the dispute were to be returned & Nand Kishor was to destroy the
main entrance of the kunj and wall it up, confining himself to his own
land. Sawai Jai Singh directed his officer Harbhakt to call representatives
(wakils) of both the disputants to Mathura, & settle with them which of
the two alternatives set by Jagannath was acceptable to both parties, &
submit a report (6 Muh.: GD).
    (2) On failure of this attempt, administrative means were adopted to
expel Nand Kishor, but this ended in fiasco on 28 March in an incident,

   1727 Purchased land in Vrindavan from one Bairagi for Rs.250
(Baindma in Braj, acc. to NAI’s report, document now presumably in NAI
collection under No. 2691: of vs 1784).
   1728 Purchased plots of land from two Chamars, Khunya and Goyasya,
in Arith for Rs.51 (vs 1785: Wright 23),
   1729 Purchased land (kyari, dressed field) in Arith from Sadaram Gaur
for Rs.61 (vs 1786), (Wright 24 and 25).
   Note: One learns from a sale-deed of ah 1146/16 Muh. (=1733) (NAI-
2691/14) that Jagannath *s/o Gobind Charan’ left behind at least two sons,
Ram Saran (q.v.) & Kashi Ram (q.v.). The land in Vrindavan, according to
this document, was purchased by Ram Saran, while Kashi Ram appeared
as his vakil before the Qazu The latter’s age was then recorded as 50 years,
so Ram Saran was presumably still older. Apparently, Jagannath followed
the custom among the Gosains in preferring Har Kishan, the son of
a predecessor (Kishan Charan) over his own sons, when choosing his
successor for both the spiritual seat & the temporal property.

JAGJIVAN DAS, s/o Gopinath.
The original madad-i ma‘dsh grant of 100 bighas, conferred under Akbar
on Madho (q.v.) (1568-94), & then reduced on account of change of
measuring rod [& introduction of bigha ilahi] to 76 bighas, 2 biswas, was
transferred after Madho’s death to Jagjivan Das [apparently as Madho’s
successor in the office of custodian of the Gopinath temple], whereafter
it continued in his line (Anonymous, parwdna, 1 Muh., citing petition of
Ram Jivan, q.v., 1 Muh.: IVS-159; & Ray Rayan’sparwdna, 4 Muh.: IVS
191-192).
    See IVS-165 (post-ah 1134/ad 1721-2) for the name of Jagjivan Dass
father.

JIV GOSA’IN / JI GUSA’IN / JIVA GOSVAMI s/o Ballabh Gosa’in.
Note: In Persian writing ‘Jiv’ and ‘Jiu’ are written the same way.
   For his father, see BALLABH Gosa’in. Jiv was a nephew (brothers
son) of RUP (&) SANATAN (letter to Mehta Sltaldas, of Shah Jahan’s

 time, undated: RD). Jiv is rather tendentiously described in (Mirza Raja)
 Jai Singhs parwdna of ah 1069/ 1659, as ‘a sevak of Rup Gosa’in* (GD).
     Purchased land at Arith/Radhakund from villagers: 1546: for Rs.20
 (ah 953: MM); 1577 (ah 985, plots of land purchased for Rs.64, Rs.20,

     1568 Raja Todar Mai reported to Emperor Akbar that Jiv Gosa’in was

 the devalas of Madan Mohan & Gobind-dev be entrusted to him ‘as it has

    1572 Bought land for Rs. 8 at Brindaban (sale-deed of ah 979: RD).

 (Radhakund), 6 plots of land for Rs.238 (RD of ah 987). Note: This

money allegedly paid amounts to Rs.242.
    1584 Received for his idol [at the Radhadamodar temple], ‘death-bed’
gift of all his property from Raghunath Das (q.v.) (Skt. will, RD/VRI; see

    1588 Received in gift (hiba) 6 plots of land in Arith from Raghunath
Das which he had purchased over the period ah 953-985/ad 1546-77 for
a total sum of Rs.238 (doc. of ah 966: orig., VRI; copy, MM). But see
entry under 1579 earlier.
    Well before 1588, Jiv Gosa’in had received gifts or bequests of all their
properties from Rup & Sanatan. Krishandas (q.v.) in a bil. statement,
S. 1694/1637, stated that Rup-Sanatan & Raghunath Gosa’in gave all
their books (postak-ha) & bills/receipts (tamassuk-ha), & their isthals at
Brindaban & Radhakund to Jiv Gosa’in (reprod. in BSOAS, XLII (2),
Plate III).
    1601 Purchased land in vill. Brindaban for Rs.16 (ah 1010: RD).
   Post-1605 Composed a short Sanskrit text celebrating the erection or
completion of the Govind-dev temple in 1590, but referring to Emperor
                   A Documentary History of the Gosains                    223

Akbar (d.1605) in the past tense and closing with this sentence: ‘This
Jiva (lit. soul) must honour the two, Shri Rupa and Sanatan, by name,
attending to the service of the Lord of Shri Vrindavan, that had been
abandoned in the Kali [age]’. It was put up as an inscription in the temple
in vs 1727 (ad 1670) (text & transl. of insc. published by G.N. Bahura
in Govindadeva: A Dialogue in Stone, ed. Margaret H. Case, New Delhi,
1996, pp. 201-03).
   Jiv Gosain had twelve retreats (kunj) in Vrindavan, in one of which
Syamanand (q.v.) lived, constantly attending on Jiv Gosa in (Gopi Ramans
statement, c.1723: CU; copy, RD). See also SYAMANAND.
   1606 dr 1608 By his will (vs 1663) and codicil thereto (vs 1665), both
in Skt., Jiv Gosa’in left all his rights of (temple) service & effects to Vilas
Das (see BILASDAS) &, after him, to Krishan Das (q.v.) (VRI/RD; text,
translation & analysis in Tarapada Mukherjee & J.C. Wright, ‘An early
testamentary document in Sanskrit’, BSOAS, XLII (2), 1979, pp. 297-
300, & Plates I-IV).
   Mukherjee & Wright (ibid., p. 315) note that the dates of Jiv’s major
writings range from 1552 to 1592, and that his reputation in Bhakti
theology was established by 1561, for which they cite Devakinandana’s
Vaishnavavandana.
   The year of his death is apparently not established, though 1618 is given
for it by Gopal Narayan Bahura (Margarate H. Case, ed., Govindadeva: A
Dialogue in Stone, New Delhi, 1993, p. 201), no authority for this being
cited by him.

KABIRAJ / KAVIRAj, KRISHAN DAS, Gosa in.
1584 Kaviraj wrote ‘death-bed’ will of Raghunath Das in Sanskrit. (RD/
VRI: BSOAS, XLII (2), 1979, p. 318).
   1608 Witnessed codicil to will of Jiv Gosa’in, signing: Krishna Das
Kaviraj (BSOAS, XLII (2), 1979, pp. 304, 310).
   He had a house, at Radhakund, which he had given to Haridas ‘Gusai’
(q.v.) (Krishan Dass bil. statement of vs 1694/1637, BSOAS, XLII (2),
Plate III, p. 316: the Persian text contains ref. to the house of‘Sri Kabiraj
Gosa’in’ but omits the ref. to its being gifted to Haridas).
   Krishan Das Kaviraj was the author of the Chaitanya-charitamrita, a
biography in verse of Chaitanya in Bengali; and he is reputed to have
served as personal attendant to Rup & Raghunath in their ‘last days’. He
also wrote Govinda-lilamrita on Radha & Krishna in Sanskrit (Sukumar
Sen, History ofBengali Literature, New Delhi, 1960, pp. 97-8).
   The family (pariwar) of Shri Kisandas Kaviraj Gusal is referred to in
224                    Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

a Braj sale-deed from Vrindavan, of 1734 (Wright 26). The purchaser
belonging to it had status high enough for Brajanand, q.v., a leading
member of the line of Rup Goswami, to sign as a witness (in Sanskrit),
hailing Radha and Govind enthroned at Vrindavan, ‘the capital of the Vraj
mandala.

KASHI RAM s/o Jagannath (q.v.) s/o Gobind Charan (q.v.)
The name of Kashi Rams grandfather given as Gobind Charan in both GD
of 1732 and NA-2691/14 of 1733 is a curious piece of inaccuracy because
Jagannath’s father was Ram Bhadr s/o Gobind Das, while Gobind Charan
was Jagannath’s cousin and predecessor as chief priest of the Govind-dev
temple.

1733) .

KISHAN CHARAN / KISAN CHARAN / KRISHAN CHARAN, s/o
Raghunath Das (Chakravarti) s/o Gobind Das.
Seventh in line of Rup Gosa’in & successor of Seo Ram Gosa’in (see
SHIV RAM) (statement, r. 1723: CU).
    1689 Received in gift (hibd) one house & two shops in Mathura from
Seo Ram (Shiv Ram, q.v.), the doc. furnishing names of the recipient’s
father, Raghunath (Chakravarti), q.v., & grandfather, Gobind Das, q.v,
(doc. of ah 1100: GD). This doc. also describes him as the ‘brother’s son’
(a slip for ‘uncles son’ presumably) of Seo Ram. This would suggest that
the descendants of Gobind Das (q.v.) and Nitanand Das (q.v.) belonged to
the same family, within which the office of the chief priest of the Govind-
dev temple rotated. Incidentally, the hibandma shows that Seo Ram still
retained a haveli, which was situated on the northern side of the gifted
haveli.
    1690 Annual cash grant of Rs.360 (Re.l per diem) sanctioned for
Kishan Charan as adhikar! of Govind-dev temple, along with a grant of
Rs.600 per annum for clothes, etc., for the two temple deities (Parwdna of
Maharaja Bishan Singh of Amber: H., bilingual, of ah 1102, pp. 166-175,
385). Shiv Ram, q.v., his predecessor, had also enjoyed this annual grant.
    1692 Mir Husaini s parwdna requiring officials of Baitul Mdl, Mathura,
not to interfere with Kishan Charan’s possession of the property (one
house & two shops) gifted to him by Seo Ram in his lifetime (36 Aur.:
CU).
    1694 (1) Muhammad Baqir’s parwdna to same effect as the prec. (37
Aur.: GD). (2) Statement for public endorsement by Krishan Charan,
 s/o Raghunath Das, s/o Bishan (!) Das to the effect that Seo Ram had
226                     Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

  gifted to him by hiba, a haveli, & two shops in Mathura. Afterwards
 owing to disturbances by the Jats (futur-i Jdtan), he went for some time
 to the Rajput country (mulk-i Rdjputiya), where he took up residence.
 In his absence, finding the houses vacant (i.e. ownerless), Ghulam Nabi,
 darogha of the Baitul Mal, resumed them to the Khalisa (imperial estate),
 and appropriated the rent. Now, however, during the *amal (term of
 administration) of Raja Bishan Singh [as jagirdar^], Krishan Charan,
 coming to Kaman, approached the darogha, but in vain. He, through his
 wakil, then obtained parwanas from Mir Husaini & Muhammad Baqir,
 Bayutat officials of Agra. The darogha has now admitted the same & put
 his seal on this statement. Many other seals of important local personages
 are affixed in endorsement (38 Aur.: NAI-2671/14). From the facts stated,
 it would appear that Kishan Charan had left Vrindavan for the ‘Rajput
 country’ after 1689 and returned to Kaman in or before 1692, when he
 obtained Mir Husaini s parwdna.
     1696 Gifted all his possessions, ‘cash & kind, havelis, gardens, retreats

non-officials and not certified by a qdzi.

1700 Her husband, Braj Kunwar (q.v.), by now dead (as reported in NAI-
2671/6).
   1704 Local officials endorsed her complaint that by closing the gate
towards her garden & opening a new one towards their own garden,
Nand Kishor (q.v.) and Bindraban (q.v.), Bairagls, followers of Syamanand
(q.v.), who were living in her garden that she held by purchase (zar

by judgement of the [qazfs] court according to law. (Mukhtar Khan’s

   1705 (1) Mukhtar Khan rescinded the above parwdna on receiving

                  A Documentary History of the Gosa ins                 229

 obtained parwdnas from Mukhtar Khan & Ziyaullah Khan (see under
 GOBIND CHARAN), Mirza Bahram Beg, faujdar of Mathura, sent one

 she occupied. She surrendered the vacant garden of Kishor Das, to be
 occupied by Nand Kishor as before, but refused to come out of the other
garden, whose gate she closed. This garden contained the samadhi of Rup
 Gosa in (q.v.). On the third day her partisan Sukh Ram (‘Sokh Ram’)
Brahman came out, struck his head against the wall & jumped from the
roof & then went to the Qazi’s court at Mathura to accuse Brajanand &
Nand Kishor of beating & robbing him. This allegation was sought to be
refuted by the officials present & the horseman & footmen sent to evict

    1706 She summoned Braj Kunwars nephew, Gopi Raman (q.v.), from
Bengal, and installed him on 30 Sha'ban 50 Aur. (16 December 1706) as
Braj Kunwars successor in adhikar (priestly function) & as heir to his seats

gardens, etc., at Mominabad (Vrindavan), & giving him a deed of gift
 (hiba) with the seal of Qazi Qutbuddin. The Braj text of the statement,

Raman, c. 1718: RD).
    (2) The above date of Gopi Raman’s installation (16 December) does not

which contains a written statement of agreement (razi-ndma) on the part

temple, as well as at other places, while handing over to Gopinath (q.v.)
properties & rights derived from the grants of‘the Barkhasan Raja’ in the
name of Radha Damodar temple. Neither of the two beneficiaries were to
lay claims over what was being given to the other (NAI-2671/19).
    Note: Tappa Barkhasan is mentioned as belonging to they^ir of Sawai
Jai Singh of Amber in 6 Muh./1724, in a parwana of that chief (GD). It
had, perhaps, a local subordinate ruler.
    1707 (1) Rustam Dil Khan’s parwana, 24 Dec., ordered eviction of Kunj
Das from lands & havelis belonging to the ruler of Amber, on demand of

Priya (1 Bah.: CU).
    (2) But on 26 Dec., Rustam Dil Khans na’ib, Sher Dil Khan issued

hasbu-l hukm, it had been found that Gobind Charan & Nand Kishor had

   Note: At this time in the eyes of the Imperial Court, Jai Singh was a
rebel, his brother Bijai Singh being now in Imperial favour.
   1710 Kunj Das appeared before Qazi Qutbuddin, claiming to represent

Matters thus became complicated with Kishan Priya turning against Gopi
Raman.
   1711 Gopi Raman & Brajanand claimed that they had previously
taken possession of Braj Kunwars haveli with Kishan Priyas permission
(razdnama) (Ghairat Khans dastak, 5 Bah.: CU). Note: Such razdndma
must have been given previous to Kishan Priyas espousal of the cause of
Gopinath, to whom she gave all her claimed property in 1710.
   1712 (1) Kishan Priyas vakil petitioned that she had entrusted the
custody (kbabardari) of her haveli & gardens to Kunj Das; yet Brajanand
& Braj La‘l (q.v., Gopi Ramans son) were unlawfully meddling with the

this was necessitated because Kunj Dass protege Gopinath had died (Jai
Singhs parwdna: C.U: ah 1124).
   (2) Brajanand continued to insist that Kishan Priya had concurred with
Gopi Ramans appointment as custodian of the Amber estate at Vrindavan
(Jai Singhs parwdna: CU of ah 1124). That this was a misrepresentation
of her current attitude is shown by her entrusting Kunj Das with the
management of her property.
   1713 Gopi Raman obtains parwdna similar in contents to Ghairat
Khan’s dastak of 5 Bah./1711 (see above), allowing claims of Gopi Raman
to the haveli in Mominabad (Vrindavan) which Gopi Raman had occupied
with Kishan Priyas agreement, but from which Kunj Das wished now
to evict him after taking possession of the attached garden (UC, of 2
Far.). Kishan Priya is not mentioned as ‘deceased’, but the probability
of her being dead is high, since otherwise she could have disowned the
agreement attributed to her by Gopi Raman.
   It is, incidentally, unlikely that she is identical with the Krishan Priya
paired with Gobind Priya (q.v.) among a group of religious persons,
including Swami Mohandas (q.v.), previously excluded from the vill.
of Radhakund, but readmitted (1713) & then again expelled (1716),
apparently at the behest of Gobind Charan.

     1641 Obtained acknowledgement from headmen of Arith/Radhakund
 that they had unlawfully sold away (for Rs.20) land which actually

     1644 (Shahjahans) farmdn: Since Akbar had entrusted the adhikar of
Madan Mohan & Gobind-dev temples, Brindaban, to Jiv Gosa’in, and
he in his lifetime had given it to his murid (disciple), Kishan Das, it is
ordered especially in view of the reported ‘integrity, honesty & modest

none should interfere in it (18 Sha.: copy, RD 133). The farmdn is referred
to in Islam Khans parwdna of the same year, which calls for entrusting the
adhikar of the temples to ‘Kisan Das, devotee (mutaqid) of Jiv Gosa’in’
(18 Sha.: VRI).
    1645-46 (1) Kishan Das, resident of Brindaban*, complained to officials
that headmen of Arith were cutting down trees & blocking the path to
the Kund, through land belonging to him (Sayyid Lutfullah’s parwdna to
Sukhdev, shiqqddr ofparg. Sahar, ah 1055: RD).
    (2) Headmen of Arith pledged before Rai Sukhdev that they would
desist from cultivating land at head of channel leading to Radhakund or
cutting down trees above the tank (ah 1055: RD).
    c.1658 Gonsai Krishan Das, a sevak ofJiv Gonsai, occupied the seat of the
latter; & Radhakund, therefore, also belongs to him. But one Brahman,
relying upon his wealth, is interfering with his rights there, which ought

pre-1659 date). Sltaldas & his kunj at Arith/ Radhakund are mentioned

   1659 Raja Jai Singhs parwdna to Gusa’in Seo Ram (Shiv Ram) (q.v.),
sevak of Sri Gobind-Dev temple: Kishan Das lives on the land & in
the retreat (kunj) of the sarkdr (of the Raja at Vrindavan) on account
of managing (the possessions of) Jiv Gusa’in, sevak of Rup Gusa’in,
adhikdri of Gobind-Dev. Out of mischief he has colluded with officials
& misrepresented the affairs of the temple (devala), etc. Jai Singh having
made representation at the Court, an imperial order to J a1 far Khan
(faujdar, Mathura] has been obtained. What status has Kishan Das that
he should by aid of fraud (sakht) interpose himself in the establishment
(sarkdr) of Gobind-Dev? Seo Ram is to consider himself as authorized to
collect offerings (beth-ddri) & manage the land & retreats (kunj-hd) of the
establishment in the same way as the preceding adhikdris of the temple did
(Jai Singh’s parwdna of ah 1069, addressed to Seo Ram, after Aurangzeb’s
accession: GD). A sad piece of humiliation for Krishan Das in his old age:
from now on the management of the Govind-dev temple passed into the
hands of the line of Rup Gosa’in.
   Krishan Das composed a commentary on Jfv’s Krishnarchanadipika
(BSOAS), XLII (2), 1979, p. 314). Not to be conffised with Kabiraj
(Kaviraj) Krishna Das (q.v.).

KUNJ DAS, Kayasth, s/o Jivan Das s/o Raghunath.
A murid (devotee) of Jiv Gosa’in (Sher Dil Khans parwdna, 1 Bah.: RD;
statement in support of Jagannath, c.1723: CU; & statement by Gopi
Raman, c.1723: CU, copy, RD); and hiba gift of 1725 (RD).
   Before & after 1700 Had been managing the establishment (kdrkhdna)
of Braj Kunwar (q.v.) & continued to do so after the latter’s death (1698-
1700) & after Gopi Raman’s (q.v.) installation as his successor in 50
Aur./1706 (statement, c.1718: RD).
    1704 Kunj Das obtained z parwdna from Mukhtar Khan, Governor of
Agra province, as wakil of Braj Kunwars widow Kishan Priya (q.v.), stating
that Nand Kishor (q.v.) & Bindraban Bairagi (q.v.) had unlawfully seized
her garden, this parwdna being one of 48 Aur., preserved in RD. This was
cancelled by a subsequent parwdna by Mukhtar Khan (of 49 Aur.: GD),
where Kunj Das is specifically mentioned as the wakil of Kishan Priya; he
is not so mentioned in the earlier parwdna, now cancelled.
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa’ins                  235

    1707 (1) Faujdar, Rustam Dil Khans parwana (24 Dec.) issued at
request of the wakil of Raja Bijai Singh: Kunj Das has misrepresented the
facts. He is to be evicted after enquiry; & the possession of the houses
(haveli-hd) &c land is to be given over to Govind Charan (q.v.) (1 Bah.:
GD; copy in CU.).
    (2) Naib faujdar, Sher Dil Khans parwana (26 Dec.): Kunj Das has
brought a hasbu-l hukm with the seal of Rustam Dil Khan referring to
his representation that his client Kishan Priya’s house (haveli) had been
occupied by Nand Kishor & Bindraban (Brindaban), Bairdgis, that they
were expelled on the strength of Mukhtar Khans (first) parwana, 8c the
house returned to her, but that now Gobind Charan & Nand Kishor
have occupied the house again with the help of Raja Jai Singhs officials.
The Emperor therefore ordered enquiry & redressal, Sher Dil Khan, in
company with the qdzi & the news reporters (sawdnih-nigar 8c waqd'i‘-
nigdr), investigated the matter & found truth in Kunj Dass statements.
Gobind Charan & Nand Kishor are to be evicted, & Kishan Priya & Kunj
Das given possession (1 Bah.: RD). See also Gopi Ramans statement,
c. 1725, about these endeavours of Kunj Das: he is said to have done so
for establishing ‘Jiv Gosa’ins right of ownership’ (no reference is made to
Kishan Priya). Kunj Das, it is added, succeeded in expelling Nand Kishor
& closed the gate the latter had built fronting his kunj (CU; copy, RD).
    (3) A fresh hasbu-l hukm issued (30 Dec.) referring to the petition of
Nand Kishor, etc., Syamanandis, to the effect that Kunj Das in Brindaban
had forcibly destroyed the petitioners’ haveli &c possessed himself of
Rs.2,000 of their money. The Emperor ordered enquiry & redressal (copy
of hasbu’l hukm of Bah. given below an official’s parwana, seal illegible:
CU; see under 1708).
    1708 Parwana, issued following hasbu-l hukm of 30 Dec. 1707, refers
to complaint of Nand Kumar & other Syamanandis that Kunj Das had
interfered with Mirza Raja Bijai Singh’s estate (milkiyai) 8c destroyed
the Syamanandis’ haveli & possessed himself of their goods. The former
Governor (suba), Mukhtar Khan, & former Diwan [of the suba], Ziya’ullah
Khan, had found Kunj Das’s claims to be baseless: he obtained a. parwana
from Rustam Dil Khan by false representations & occupied the house
by collusion with the latter’s naib (deputy), Sher Dil Khan. He is to be
evicted, & Nand Kishor is to be put in occupation (seal illegible, date lost:
CU). Statement in support ofJagannath, (q.v.) c.1725 (CU), also recounts
that Kunj Das, a murid of Jiv Gosa in, expelled Nand Kishor & other
Syamanandis; but Brajanand (q.v.) succeeded in re-installing them. See
also under GOBIND CHARAN, BRAJANAND, & NAND KISHOR.
236                     Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

    1706-10 Some time after Gopi Ramans installation (50 Aur./1706), he
suspected Kunj Das of misappropriating large amounts in cash & goods,
& called for accounts. Kunj Das responded by bringing Braj Kunwars
daughter’s son, Gopinath (q.v.) from Bengal, & sought to set him up as
Braj Kunwar’s successor through misrepresentations. Gopi Raman tried to
 thwart Kunj Das’s schemes by obtaining sanads at much cost (Statement
 on Gopi Ramans behalf, c. 1718: RD).
    1710 Kunj Das, ‘s/o Jivan Das s/o Raghunath’, appeared before Qazi
 Qutbuddin, claiming to represent Kishan Priya (q.v.), & recording her
 act of gift (hiba) of all her property, consisting of 12 plots of land, haveli,
    garden in Brindaban, a house at Kaman, & lands in Arith, Nandesar
& Govardhan, to her daughters son Gopinath (q.v,). Kunj Das’s personal
description (huliya) in the document gives his age as 50 years (RD, of 4
Bah.)
    1711 Kunj Das Kayath & Gopinath had allegedly seized the house
in land belonging to Raja Jai Singh’s estate, where Gopi Raman was
permitted to reside and where his uncle Braj Kunwar used to live, &
also took possession of Braj Kunwar’s money & goods. Emperor ordered
enquiry and redressal. Hasbu’l Hukm to Shaikh Abu Said, ‘amil [& amin
 (revenue collector and assessor) of Princess Imtiyaz Mahal]: GD 5 Bah.,
& Ghairat Khans dastak: CU, 5 Bah.
    1712 (1) Brajanand secured from Raja Jai Singh of Amber the
appointment of Gopi Raman as the custodian of the Amber estate at
Vrindavan. Though Gopinath was now dead, Kunj Das was still trying to
subvert Gopi Raman’s custodianship having obtained an imperial sanad in
favour of Gopinath: Jai Singh’s parwanas of ah 1124, three of which were
issued on the same date and with the same text to three different officials
of his (CU).
    (2) Kishan Priya complained that she had entrusted her own haveli &
gardens to the custody (khabardan) of Kunj Das, but Brajanand & Braj
La‘l (q.v.) were unlawfully meddling with the property (Asafu’ddaulas
parwdna, 1 Jahandar Shah, RD).
    1713 (1) A dastak of a high official (seal unclear) upholds Gopi Ramans
claims to the haveli of Braj Kunwar against the pretensions of Kunj Das of
Kayath caste (of 2 Far.: CU).
    (2) Kunj Das is said to have falsely urged Gopinath’s claims to the
custody of 135 bighas of grant & other lands of Raja Jai Singh at Brindaban
to the detriment of the rights of Gobind Charan & Gopi Raman (Saiyyid
‘Abdullah Khan’s parwdna, 2 Far.: ^azf-certified copy, CU).
    1717 Purchased 4 plots of land in Vrindavan for Rs. 199, the sale-deed
describing Kunj Das as ‘s/o Braj Kunwar s/o Nand Kunwar’, which shows
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa’lns                237

that he was now directly claiming to be the adopted son and successor of
Braj Kunwar (q.v.) in the line of Jiv Gosa’in (RD 82, of ah 1129).
    c.1718 Gopi Raman succeeded in foiling Kunj Das’s scheme to put
Gopinath in possession of the haveli in dispute. Subsequently, Gopinath
had died of smallpox, & Kunj Das thereupon put forward his own claim to
headship (sardart), though being a Kayath his pretensions were contrary
to ‘Hindu!’ custom. He had now taken possession of the haveli excluded
Gopi Raman from it. (Statement, bilingoal, undated, on behalf of Gopi
Raman, c.1718: RD 30).
    1723 Kunj Das nominated his nephew Radhavinod Das (q.v.) to succeed
him with all his property (Hindi will, RD/VRI; discussed, BSOAS, XLII
(2), 1979, pp. 310, 320).
    1725 (1) Jagannath (q.v.) complained at the Court against Gopi Raman
6 Kunj Das for harassing him. Raja Jai Singh was ordered to settle the
matter (hasbu’l hukm, of 7 Muh. GD). Jagannath specifically accused both
of forging three farmdns in order to oust him from his seats (amkina')
 (Vazir Qamaruddin Khans parwdna addressed to ‘Ali Naqi Khan Rasikh,
7 Muh.: GD). This suggests a reconciliation had by now taken place
between Kunj Das & Gopi Raman, as is also borne out by the way Kunj
Das is favourably referred to as ‘a murid [follower] of Jiv Gosa’in’ in Gopi
Raman’s statement of c.1725 (CU, copy in RD 27).
    (2) Kunj Das ‘s/o Braj Kunwar (!) s/o Raghunath’ gave in gift his
haveli, with its appurtenances (marafiq) in vill. Brindaban to Radhabinod
 [Radhavinod, q.v.] ‘s/o Parsottam s/o Jivan’. His own huliya on the
 hibandma gives his age as 68 years (RD 141 of ah 1137/7 Muh.)
    1731 Kunj Das ‘s/o Braj Kunwar s/o Kishandas’ (spiritual line!)
 purchased from Koli s/o Sundar 3 bighas, 12 biswas of land in Vrindavan
 for Rs.72. Deed dated 19 Sha‘ban 13 ry Muh (9 March 1731) (RD 91).
    1733 It is not clear whether Kunj Das was now alive since not he
 but his (adopted) son Gusai Radhabinod, now appears as ‘adhikari of
 Radhamadanmohan (temple)’ at Vrindavan (Wright 28, of vs 1790).
    1736 Kunj Das was by now dead. On the prayer of his chela
 [& nephew], Radha-binod, zamindars were asked to pay to the latter
 the revenue due from Kunj Dass grant of 30 bighas in vill. Atas, parg.
 Sahar, or, demarcating it, hand over equivalent waste land to Radhabinod
 for getting it cultivated (letter of Rai Nanda to Sadanand, 18 Muh.:
 RD).

MURLIDHAR, Gusai, s/o Ganga Ram, Gusai.
Names of father and son preceded by the designation gusai in the Braj
portion of the bilingual document, IVS 217 A-B, dated 25 Rajab 35
Sha./28 April 1658. Murlidhar here acknowledges that land with a kunj,
10 biswas in area, in Arith, was purchased by him in the name of his
father, Ganga Ram, from Gobind-das, Gopinath and Mohan, Brahman
pujarls, for Rs.24; but that price was paid by Sitaldas (the verb used
implying much respect), to whom, therefore, the property should belong.
Any claim on the basis of Ganga Rams name in the sale-deed (tamassuk),
raised by any heir of Ganga Ram to that property, would, therefore, be
false (daroght). Apparently, Sitaldas was such a well-reputed man that
further details of him (parentage, etc.) were not considered necessary.

NAND KISHOR, Bairagt, Syamanandi s/o (?) Braj Jananand s/o
Nitanand.
Described as son ofBraj Jananand (q.v.) s/o Nitanand, in a Qazi’s judgement,
ah  1136/ad 1723 (GD); but the relationship with Braj Jananand seems
to have been spiritual rather than of blood, since according to another
statement, he was really Braj Jananand’s ‘servant and agent’ (khadim o
gumdshta), left at Brindaban after the latter’s visit (1703) (statement in
support of Jagannath, c.1723. GD). According to yet another source, he
was a murid (disciple) of Babu (q.v.) & Lalu (q.v.) (murids of Syamanand)
who deviated from the path of their preceptor (Gopi Raman’s statement,
c.1725: CU, copy: RD).
   1704 Nand Kishor & Bindraban (Brindaban), Bairagis, murids of
Syamanand, (q.v.) who had been living in a garden belonging to Kishan
Priya (q.v.), were trying to seize it (Mukhtar Khan’s parwdna, 48 Aur:
RD).
   1705 (1) As wakil of Gobind Charan Gosa in, Nand Kishor successfully
contested Kishan Priya’s claims before Mukhtar Khan, by pointing out
that the land belonged to Raja Jai Singh, not Kishan Priya; & Mukhtar
Khan thereupon rescinded the previous year’s parwdna (Parwdna, 49 Aur.:
GD).
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa’ins                241

    (2) When local officials sought to implement the parwana obtained by
him, Nand Kishor was able to occupy the garden of Kishor Das (?), but
Kishan Priya refused to come out of the other garden. The siege continued
for three days without effect. On the third day Nand Kishor, along with
Brajanand (wakil of Gobind Charan), was accused of beating & robbing
Kishan Priya’s partisan, Sukh Ram (‘Sokh Ram’) Brahman (Statement of
members of official party 49 Aur.: GD). See Chapter 5 of this volume for
a fuller account of the incident.
    1707 (1) By now the haveli under dispute had been ‘unlawfully’ occupied
by Gobind Charan & Nand Kishor, with the assistance of officials of Raja
Jai Singh (Sher Dil Khan’s parwana 2 Dec RD).
    (2) In his risala (note) Raja Jai Singh’s brother and rival, Bijai Singh
(11 November), addressing Nand Kishor, held Gobind Charan to be the
custodian of the Amber estate at Vrindavan and stated that the kunj in
which Syamanand had lived, had been converted into a haveli by Kishordas
Rajawat, who then handed it over to Radhakishan Syamanandi (q.v.).
On the latter’s death he installed Nand Kishor therein. Since the original
 kunj was built on land held by Amber rulers, Bijai Singh cofirmed these
arrangements (risala of ah 1119: CU). But contrary official directions
were also received. Na’ib faujdar, Sherdil Khan, directed (26 Dec.), after
 receipt of a hasbu-l hukm for enquiry & redressal, issued upon Kishan
 Priya’s complaint (through Kunj Das), that Nand Kishor & Bindraban be
 expelled from the haveli (Parwana, 1 Bah.: RD). Meanwhile the Faujdar,
 Rustam Dil Khan, had issued (24 Dec.) a parwana entertaining the
 complaint of Govind Charan against Kunj Das & rescinding his previous
parwana (1 Bah.: GD).
     (3) A hasbu-l hukm was issued on complaint of Nand Kishor that Kunj
 Das had destroyed his haveli & interfered with Raja Jai Singhs estate;
 it ordered enquiry & redressal (CU). The upshot seems to have been
 that Nand Kishor Syamanandi was installed in the kunj, garden or haveli,
 which was disputed between the lines of Rup Gosa in (Gobind Charan)
 & Jiv Gosa in (Kishan Priya & Kunj Das). See statements in support of
 Jagannath, c.1723, made before & after Qazi’s judgement (CU & GD). See
 also Gopi Ramans statement, c. 1725 (CU; copy, RD). The land concerned
 contained the kunj where Syamanand had lived in the time of Jiv Gosa in,
 together with another plot, 12 biswas in all, which Gobind Charan had
 given either by hiba (gift) or lease (‘ariyatari) to Braj Jananand, q.v.
     1719-20 After the death of Braj Jananand away in Orissa, & of Gobind
 Charan, & during the regime of the Namak Hardman (Sayyid Brothers,
  1719-20), Nand Kishor began to claim a plot comprising 12 biswas of
242                    Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

  land for his own use on the basis of a claimed gift from Gobind Charan
  to Braj Jananand. In this he obtained assistance of officials, Bhlm Singh
 & Jogul Arham (?), etc., by bribery & false papers. He built a new big
 gate, without permission of Jagannath, Gobind Charan s successor, &
 established a campsite for horses, cows, carriage (bahal), etc., with trees
 grown taller so that ten to twelve or more of his followers (Agarguria =
 Oriya?) now stayed there (Statement for public endorsement on behalf of
 Jagannath; CU, undated, but preceding Qazi’s judgement for which see
 under 1723).
     1722 (1) Nand Kishor was accused by Gopi Raman (‘Gopi Rawan Das’)
 (q.v.) of trying to seize the haveli in Brindaban belonging to Kishan Priya
 (q.v.), which previously Nand Kishor had been obliged to surrender to the
 latter after she had proved her claims. Now again he & Gobind Charan (!)
 have taken possession of the haveli, seized the money & goods kept there,
 & altered entrances. Inquiry & relief ordered (Copy of Nizamu’l Mulks
parwdna, 4 Muh.: RD). [Gobind Charan was actually dead by this time].
     (2) Accused, along with Syamanandis & Gobind Charan (!), of trying
 to usurp land & buildings in Brindaban, which by lawful inheritance
 should have come to Gopi Raman, the accuser. Inquiry & relief ordered
 (Nizamu’l Mulks parwdna, orig.; RD).
     1723 (1) The Qazi of Mathura heard suit of Jagannath (wakil:
Brajanand) against Nand Kishor (wakil: Syam Das, q.v.) in the dispute
over 12 biswas of land. On Nand Kishor s behalf, a doc. of hiba of the land
to Braj Jananand by Gobind Charan, was produced; but this was held
to be invalid, since the property (both by imperial grant & such as was
acquired) belonged to the Raja of Amber, and Gobind Charan, being in
the nature of its custodian, could not gift it away on his own. Therefore
Nand Kishor was to be evicted & Jagannath installed as custodian on
behalf of Raja Jai Singh (GD of 5 Muh.; copy, Jaipur),
    (2) Upon the qdzi’s judgement, Nand Kishor s wakil agreed to vacate the
garden & close the big gate. He went out of Brindaban with Nand Kishor
& left the place (Statement with seal of Malukchand, Naib Faujdar, and
signatures of local officials: GD).
    1724 (1) Nand Kishor did not in fact vacate the land under dispute, so
now Sawai Jai Singh appointed two other Gosai’ins Syam Charan (q.v.)
and Subal Das (q.v.) along with Jagannath to settle the dispute. The ,
terms Jagannath offered were: either that Nand Kishor vacated the land
& obtained the current price of the building materials from Jagannath or
carried them away; or that he stayed on the land, after destroying the big
gate & walling it, and undertook to depart whenever asked to do so by
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa ’ins                 243

Jagannath. Syam Charan had got the door of Nand Kishors kunj closed,
but this did not satisfy Jagannath (Maharaja Jai Singhs parwdna, of 6
Muh., GD).
   (2) Apparently, on the failure of this attempt at reconciliation, an effort
was made to expel Nand Kishor from the land and kunj he had occupied.
Spurred by parwanas from Raja Aya Mai (Minister at the Court) and
Raja Sawai Jai Singh, Harbhakt Rai, Deputy-Governor of Agra Province,
pressed his deputy at Mathura, Mehta Gokuldas, to enforce Nand Kishor’s
expulsion from the property illegally occupied by him, with a view to
handing it over to Jagannath Gosa’in. On 28 March 1724 a posse of
horsemen (sawdranj was sent to bring Nand Kishor to Mathura to have
the case decided in the court (‘adalat), attended by imperial officials.
When the horsemen laid siege to the gate of Nand Kishor’s kunj & land,
the servants of Braj Lal (q.v.), son of Gopi Raman (q.v.), came to his
assistance and, with others from inside, physically resisted the horsemen.
A bairagi with a dagger (jamdhar) threatened to kill anyone who came
near and then kill himself. After meeting such ‘disobedience and unruly
conduct’ the horsemen withdrew to report to Harbhakt Rai—Statement
endorsed by Sultan Khan, kotwal of Mathura, agents of sawanih-nigar,
wdqia-nigdr &c harkdra (all official news-reproters), and an official of
Sawai Jai Singh, with their seals, and signatures of several witnesses in
Nagari (CU).
   It seems that there was some settlement made between Nand Kishor
& Jagannath for in 1725 there was now a dispute over the same property
between Gopi Raman & Jagannath, and also between Gopi Raman &
Nand Kishor.
   1725 The same land with kunj &c big gate at Vrindavan had also been
the subject of dispute between Gopi Raman & Jagannath. The latter’s
predecessor, through Brajanand, had got possession through a parwdna
during Mukhtar Khans time (1705-06). Kunj Das (q.v.) had then set
matters right by securing the claims of Jiv Gusa in’s line & getting Nand
Kishor Syamanandi expelled from it & destroying the gate he had built,
during the term of Rustam Dil Khan as faujddr. Now, however, Nand
Kishor had established himselfforcibly & set up the gate again. The matter
being brought to the notice of Raja Sawai Jai Singh, at his direction, the
na’ibfaujdar Kirpa Ram, enquired into the rights to the kunj, Sc finding
Gopi Raman to be the rightful possessor of the property of Jiv Gusa in,
expelled Nand Kishor along with his followers & goods & installed Gopi
Raman therein (Gopi Raman’s statement for public endorsement, CU;
copy: RD). This was what Sawai Jai Singh had also ordered: Nand Kishor,
244                    Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

disciple {murid) of Syamanand Bairagi, was to be expelled from the kunj
belonging to Gopi Raman in vill. Brindaban, and the kunj was to be
handed over to the latter (Jai Singhs parwana, 7 Muh.: RD: VRI, SI. no.
4b, acc. no. 28). Here Nand Kishor departs from our record.

&, throwing him at Braj Kunwars feet, got him back into the kunj in

RADHAVINOD DAS, Gusai, s/o Parsottam s/o Jivan (hibanama of

1723 Nominated as heir by Kunj Das, q.v., his uncle (RD/VRI ser. 18,

   1725 ‘Radhabinod, s/o Parsottam s/o Jivan’ obtained from his uncle,
Kunj Das, his haveli (house) with appurtenances in Vrindavan, by legal gift
(hiba). In huliya in the hibanama (RD), he is described as pockmarked and
of about 37 years of age.
   1729 Had laid claim to half-share in chaudhurdi and zamlndafi of
pargana Shahabad, siiba of Bengal, on account of his father Parsottam
holding this office: now confirmed by a hasbu’l hukm issued by the Wazlr

   1736 Radhabinod, chela of Kunj Das, dec., petitioned seeking issuance
of an order to zamindars to let him collect revenue from 30 bighas of
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa’ins                  247

grant in vill. Atas, parg. Sahar. The consequential order stipulates that
payment due should be made by zamindars, but no deduction, on that
account be allowed (mujra) from state revenue. If grant-land had not been
previously demarcated, this should be done now, but only wasteland was
to be transferred, not cultivated land. So no reduction in revenue demand
on this village on this score was to be made, and the grantee was not to be
allowed to obtain cultivated land through collusion with zamindars. (Rai
Nandas letter to Sadanand, 18 Muh: RD).
   c.1741 Land purchased in Vrindavan for Rs.53 + Rs.13 by ‘Gusai
Radhavinod, son of Shri Gusai Kunj Das, of the family (parivar) of Shri Jiv
Gusai, adhikari of Shri Radhamadanmohan [temple]’ (Wright 28). Since
the last digit of the Samvat year 179x is not clear, the doc. can belong to a
whole decade ad 1732-42. Wright prefers 1741 on orthographic grounds.
If so, as time passed Radhavinods pretensions seem to have increased
for him to claim, first, Kunj Das as his father, &, then, Jiv Gosa in as
ancestor; therely attaining, the status of a Brahman, though he, like Kunj
Das, was a Kayasth by birth,

RAGHUNATH DAS, Gosa in.
1537-8 His purchase of plots of land in Arith/ Radhakund began from this
year (vs 1595: W.1A), though the reference to ‘rupee’ (rupaiya) in terms
of which the price is stated in the doc. suggests the sale-deed was actually
drawn up much later. The other Braj sale-deeds are of the following years:
 1545 (vs 1602: W.l); 1548 (?) (vs 1605?: W.2), while Raghunaths own
deed of gift to Jiv Gosa in (q.v.) lists 6 plots, allegedly purchased by him
in the following years; ah 953/1546 (2 plots); ah 970/1562 (one plot); ah
985/1570 (3 plots), the total cost, Rs.238. (Doc. of ah 966/1588'. VRI;
copy, MM).
     /5<?4 Made a ‘death-bed’ gift ofall his possessions to the ‘idol worshipped
by Jiva’ (Radha Damodar temple), describing himself as ‘zealous for the
service of Radhakunda’ (extract transcribed & translated, BSOAS, XLII
 (2), 1979, p. 318).
    1588 Gifted by hiba to Jiv Gosa’in (all the?) six plots of land owned by
him in Arith/ Radhakund, along with deeds relating thereto (ah 966:
VRI; copy, MM).
    According to Kishandas’s (q.v.) statement (vs 1694/ad 1637), Raghunath
Gosa in, together with Rup (&) Sanatan, gave all their books (postak-ha),
bills & docs, (tamassuk-hd) & their isthals of Brindaban & Radhakund
to Jiv Gosain (Bit, GD; reproduced: BSOAS, XLII (2), 1979, Plate III).
    A statement of zamindars & muqaddams of Arith (vs 1770/ad 1713)
claimed that ‘Jati Raghunath Das Godiya’ came, cut the jungle, settled
here, purchased land from their forebears, &, enlightened by Thakuranljl
(Radha), discovered Radhakund & Krishankund. He gave all his property
& papers to Haridas Gosai (q.v.), adhikdri of Govind-dev, in the line
(pari war) of Radha-Gadadhar (Wright 17). This, of course, contradicts
Kishandas’s statement (above) that Raghunath had gifted all his possessions
to Jiv Gosa in, but is, perhaps, a late tradition designed to bolster the
claims of the line of Rup Gosa in (q.v.) as aginst those of Jiv Gosa in over
properties in Arith (Radhakund).
    It is possible that a kunj of Raghunath Das Gosa in at Arith/Radhakund
is referred to in a doc. of ah 1080/ad 1669, only the second portion of
his name being given. Cf. the omission of the initial name in the Braj text
of Krishan Dass statement of vs 1694/1637 (BSOAS, XLII (2), Plate III
&p. 316).

RAM JIVAN Gosa’in s/o Syam s/o Gopal Das s/o Anant s/o Jagjivan
Das, heir of Madho, q.v. \adhikdri, Gopinath temple], Grandson of

 bighas, now reduced to 76 bighas 2 biswas in vill. Raj pur near Vrindavan,
originally held by Madho, now confirmed in Ram Jivan s name (Parwdna,
 1 Muh: IVS 159).

parwdna, 4 Muh: IVS 191-192).

of Gopinath temple from Raja Ram sdhukdr (banker), native of Bengal
 (transfer deed, 7 Muh: IVS 205).
    1726 It was during his stewardship of the Gopinath temple that the
 Vazir Qamaruddln Khan issued a parwdna confirming the temple s land­
grants in Vrindavan: 142 bighas by farmdns, &c 100 bighas by parwanas
from officials (IVS 177 & 200).
    Much of the other information about Ram Jivan comes from documents
relating to a dispute with Devakl Nandan Gosa’in. As reconstructed
from IVS 158, 167, 171, & 187, the dispute originated thus: Kanhar
(or Kandhar) Das Kayath (q.v.), sevak of Gopinath, came to Vrindavan,
purchased a plot of waste land adjacent to the ahata (compound) of the
Gopinath temple & built on it a haveli or masonry kunj at the instance of
‘Gusa’in Jiu’ (the then adhikari of the temple). When after residing there
for some time he left for some other place (pardes), he made an offering
of it to the Gopinath temple. Thereafter, when, during Aurangzeb’s
reign, the idol [thakur] was removed to Kaman, the building went into
desrepair, & only a platform remained. Sixty years later [in the late 1720’s
presumably] ‘Gusa’in Jiu [the then custodian of the temple] built the
haveli anew. Some unoccupied land remained between its courtyard & the
road; & Devakl Nandan [Gosa’in] claimed this was his land purchased
from his maternal uncle Lachhl Ram. When Fateh Singh & other panch
asked kim to produce the sale-deed, he said it was with Lachhl Ram.
250                     Braj Bhum in Mughal Times

When the latter came back from the Panjab, he denied having it. But later
he agreed to the falsehood, and in ah 1141/ad 1728-29, Devakl Nandan
took him to Qazi Iftikharu’ddin and obtained the Qazi’s seal on the sale-
deed. When Ram Jivan heard of this, he obtained endorsement from the
panch, zamindars & other inhabitants on a statement to the effect that the
land belonged to the Gopinath temple, & Devakl Nandan s claim upon it
was false (IVS 171 of 21 Muh/1739 & IVS 187 A & B).
    1731 Chandar Sen (q.v.) represented Ram Jivan before Qazi
Iftikharu’ddin, & the qdzi sent a chobdar (mace-bearer) to demand the
sale-deed bearing his seal from Devakl Nandan. The latter pledged himself
to produce this within two weeks (Iqramama, ah 1144: IVS 158, where
Devakl Nandan is described as of the Brahman caste).
    1734 The dispute came again before Qazi Iftikharu’ddin, who enquired
from the panch, zamindars, &: residents, & scrutinised the sanads of Raja
Sawai Jai Singh. He concluded that the open land between the compound
of Gopinath temple and the road belonged to the (adhikaris of) Gopinath
temple, and the deed bearing his own seal obtained by Devakl Nandan was
null & void (Qazis judgement, 16 Muh: IVS 167).
    1739 [Ram Jivan sought] endorsement for the further statement that
Maharaja Sawai Jai Singh entrusted the decision on the dispute to Diwan
Bidyadhar, who sent two horsemen, and they, assembling the faujdar
(thanadar?) of the place, Kishan Chand, & all the panch, found Devakl
Nandan s claims to be false, & ruled that Gusain Ram Jivan should receive
a sanad from the Diwani (21 Muh: IVS 171).
    IVS 187, which is undated, is a document issued by an unnamed
‘adhikari of the Gopinath temple’, seeking public endorsement for the
complaint that Jodharam, brother of Devakl Nandan, presuming upon his
being in service of Kunwar Suraj Mai [the Jat chief], broke the lock of the
haveli, took away all goods in it, & pulled it down.
    Undated: A zamindar, Raghu Dev, chaudhuri of pargana Mahmud
Shah! [sarkdr Mahmudabad,] in Bengal had made in writing a grant of
vill. Sarmast Diya in that pargana to the Gopinath temple at Brindaban;
yet the zamindars men did not allow the temples agent (gumasbta) access
to the vill. A local official, addressed as ‘Rai Sahab’, is requested to direct
the zamindars to follow past practice & not meddle with the temple
agents proceedings. Favour for ‘Ram Jivan Gosal, adhikari of the temple
 [Gopinath]’ is also solicited. (Letter to ‘Rai Sahab’ from X: IVS 152).

RUP Gosa’in, adhikari of Govind-dev temple.
‘Rup & Haridas (q.v.), by name, preceptors of the preceptor of Swami
Brajanand, (q.v.), having renounced the world at the time when the
village of Brindaban in the said pargana (Mathura) was full of jungle
& uninhabited, established their residence here. They instituted the
headship & custom of the mendicants (juqraydn) of their sect, so that
                  A Documentary History of the Gosa’ins                 253

becoming recluses, they would not touch (wordly) desire & would engage
themselves in the worship of Divinity (Samdiyat) & in undertaking
pilgrimages (sayahat) in Braj Bhum. They pursue mendicancy (gadai) &
take residence in Braj Bhum alone, & entertain mendicants, Hindu &
Muslim, who come to them hungry’ (Mukhtar Khans parwana, 48 Aur/
ad 1704: GD). Rup Gosain is designated adhikari of Govind-dev temple
in Jai Singh’s parwana of ah 1069/ad 1659 (GD), where Jiv Gosa in (q.v.)
is said to have been his sevak.
    1568 By this year Rup Gusa’in had entrusted the adhikar of the devalas

not establish, that Rup was alive when the farman was issued.

Sanatan & Raghunath Gosa in had given all their books (postak-ha), bills
& documents (tamassuk-ha), &c isthals at Vrindavan & Radhakund to Jiv
Gosa in (BSOAS, XLII (2), 1979, Plate III & p. 316).

(at Vrindavan), then under occupation of Kishan Priya (q.v.), widow of
Braj Kunwar, of the line of Jiv Gosa’in (49 Aur: GD).
    Rup was succeeded as adhikari of Govind-dev temple by Haridas (q.v.).

RUP KABIRAJ Bairagi.
1711 He had lived at the [Radha] Kund isthal (Arith) of Gobind-Dev,

parwana, in response to Jagannath’s complaint, ah 1123: GD). In the Braj

                            Appendix



NOTE ON SUCCESSION CUSTOMS IN THE CHAITANYA SECT OF
VRINDAVAN: THE LINE OF RUP GOSA’IN

The Chaitanya sect at Vrindavan mainly revolved around the rituals and
temples established by the brothers Rup and Sanatan and their nephew Jiv
Gosa’in. All three led celibate lives and left behind no children. Rup seems
to have held the office of chief priest of the Govind-dev temple, whose
duties were subsequently exercised by Haridas, who was an associate of Jiv
Gosa in. There is no direct indication that his two successors Gobind Das
and Nitanand Das were related to him. That the latter two were brothers is
inferred from a later reference to the kinship existing between Shiv Ram,
grandson of Nitanand Das, and Kishan Charan, grandson of Gobind Das.
The succession now passed from Nitanand to his son Radhakishandas
Chakravarti and then to his grandson Shiv Ram, the father-to-son norm
being followed.
   But Shiv Ram’s choice of successor fell on Kishan Charan, the
grandson of Gobind Das. Kishan Charan chose not his son, Har Kishan,
but his brother Gobind Charan, who in turn chose his cousin Jagannath
as his successor. During this period Shiv Ram’s grandson Brajanand
became a prominent figure in the Govind-dev establishment, but he was
not destined to occupy the head priests office; Jagannath’s successor, as
chosen by him, was a less distant relative, Har Kishan, son of Kishan
Charan. In apparently a return favour, Har Kishan seems to have chosen
as his successor, Ram Saran, son of Jagannath.
   The family tree of the Gosa ins of Rup s line from Gobind Das onwards,
as established from our documents, is reproduced below. The numbers in
brackets precede the names of those who succeeded to the office of high
priest of the Govind-dev temple after Haridas, and indicate their relative
positions in the order of succession. They all belong to the same family,
but a direct father-to-son succession occurs only twice out of nine cases of
                                    Appendix                                 261

succession after Gobind Das entered upon the priestly office. The period
during which a person is shown by our documents to have been alive is
indicated within brackets after his name. As noted above, it is probable,
but not certain, that (3) and (4) were sons of (2).

Irfan Habib (b. 1931). Educated till M.A. at
Aligarh, with D.Phil. from Oxford 1958, he
taught from 1953 to 1994 at Aligarh where he is
now Professor Emeritus. His works include The
Agrarian System of Mugful India, 1656—1707 (1963,
revd. edn.,4999 & 201.4); An Atlas of the Mughal
Umpire (1983); Essays in Indian History, Towards a
Marxist Perception (1995); Medieval India: the Study
of a Civilisation (2008); Economic History of Medieval
India, 1200E-1500 (2011); and Atlas of Ancient
Indian History (with Paiz Habib) (2012). He has
published twelve monographs in the People’s
History of India series, two of these jointly
authored. He has co-editcd Cambridge Economic
History of India, Vol. I (1982); UNESCO’s
History of Humanity, Vol. IV (1999) and Vol. V
(2000); and UNESCO’s History of Central Asia,
Vol. V (2003).

Tarapada Mukherjee (1928-90). Educated,
Calcutta and Visva-Bharati, he obtained his
doctoral degree (1959) from the School of
Oriental and African Studies (SOAS), London,
with a thesis on Brajbuli poetry. Having already
published an anthology of Bengali poetry (1954),
he undertook (with T.W. Clerk) a translation of
the novel Pathar Panchali (1969). In 1971, came
his study of Krishna Kirtan, and later, with the
collaboration of Sukumar Sen, a critical edition
of Krishnadas Kaviraj’s Chaitanyacharitamrita
(1988). In the meantime he organized the
project of tracking down and photographing
all old documents in possession of temples and
individuals in the Braj region. Based on this
material, he published four articles (one jointly
withj.C. Wright and the rest with Irfan Habib).
